NEWS FROM ABROAD. An anecdote about Lord Rosebery is going the round of some French and German papers. It is alleged that once, while crossing from Liverpool to Dublin, or vice versa, Lord Rosebery lost his favourite dog" Mutton " overboard. "Stop, captain, stop the steamer !" cried Lord Rosebery. But the captain replied that he was only allowed to stop if a man fell overboard. " Oh, that can soon be arranged," answered Lord Rosebery, and jumped into the sea. Of course the captain had to order "Stop," and the Peer and his dog were taken up safe and sound. JEWELLERY ROBBERY AT WIGAN. ARREST AT WARRINGTON. On Thursday week at Wigan,a young man named Henry Simons was charged with breaking and entering a lock- up shop in Wallgate, Wigan, and stealing therefrom 84 watches, 120 rings, and other articles of jewellery of the value of £l2O, the property of Mr. John Dickinson, pawnbroker.—The Chief-constable stated that Mr. D,ck- inson's shop was broken into on Friday night last and the articles mentioned in the charge stolen. On Wed- nesday prisoner visited a pawnbroker's shop in Warrington and attempted to pawn a watch which was identified as belonging to Mr. Dickinson.—Prisoner said he could give a satisfactory account of his whereabouts on the night of the robbery. At the time he was in Rochdale. The watch was given to him by a young man, who asked him to pawn it.—Prisoner Was remanded for a week in order that further inquiries might be made. BETTING RAIDS IN YORKSHIRE. FORTY-TWO PERSONS ARRESTED. On Thursday night week,the county police of the Dews- bury Division, acting under Uri direction of Superinten- dent Midgley, made a raid on the Royal Hotel, Batley, the Tablot Hotel, Batley, and the Navigation Inn, Mir- field. Fifty censtables took part in the raid, and alto- gether forty-two persons were arrested under the Gaming Act, including the landlord of each hotel, the landlady of the Navigation Inn, and three well-known book- makers—William Greaves. of Batley ; James Dailey, Batley; and John Mick, Mirfield. The last-named had above £7O in his possession, and the other bookmakers had also a large amount of money as well as betting. books in their pockets. The Batley defendants were taken to the Town Hall in waggonettes, and formally remanded until the 24th, and bail was allowed. The Mirfield defendants were remanded until the 21st. The arrest caused quite a sensation in Batley, and several thousand people assembled in front of the Town Hall during the time formal evidence was being taken. -4,- A PRACTICAL ANARCHIST. _- An imident has occurred in a little village near Milan, in Italy, which has been the subject of discourse in all the country-side. The labourers on a property belong- ing to a rich proprietor named Maj arrived at the manor house with their carts full of corn to make the consign- ment. They were received by Mats son, a young man of twenty-three years of age, who, standing at the gate of the courtyard, addressed them as follows—" Why do you bring us the corn which you have cultivated with so much trouble ? Do you not know that the grain you have sown, cultivated, and cut is yours, just as the ground on which we are born is ours ? Go home, go home ; keep the corn, and shout with me Long live Anarchy !' " The labourers looked at each other stupe- fied,but the young man insisted, and at last the peasants took the corn away, shouting" Long live Anarchy !" The next day the proprietor returned, and, with the help of he earabineers and some police from Milan, forced the 'peasants to return the corn. The son thought it pru- dent to leave the place and go to Lugano for an in- definite period.

WEEKLY REPORT ON THE HEALTH OF WARRINGTON. Week ending Bth September, 1894. During the week, which was the 36th of the current year, the following births and deaths were registered : Births. Deaths. Warrington 49 20 Latchford 3 5 Total 52 25 The excess of births over deaths was 27. The death- rate from all causes was equal to 23.2 per 1,000, against 13.8 the average of the previous four weeks, and against 29•2 the corresponding week of last year. The death' rate from the seven chief zymotic diseases was equal to 4.6 per 1,000, against 2 the average rate of the four previous weeks,and against 66 the corresponding week ot 1893. The ages of mortality were- -0-1 1-5 5-15 15-25 25-60 60 up. 6 2 1 8 3 2C; 3 1 1 5 25 The deaths included-3 from diarrhcea, 1 from whoop- ing cough, 1 frorn measles, 1 from phthisis, 1 from cancer, 2 from old age, 2 from premature birth, 4 from lung disease, 2 from liver disease, 1 from brain disease, 1 from drowning. The following cases of infectious disease have been reported during the week :—Scarlet fever 9 cases, enteric fever 1 case. Total number of patients in hospital September Bth METEOROLOGY.—The mean temperature was 52 Fab,. and therefore 6 deg. lower than the preceding week. The maximum temperature 61 F., was recorded on the 2nd inst., and the minimum temperature 40 F., on the 4th inst. The rainfall measured I inch. The general direction- was N.E., the reading of the Anemometer denoting 76Er miles. JOHN H. GORNALL, Medical Officer of Health. LYIIII. WESLEYAN HARVEST FESTIVAL.—The harvest festival' in connection with the Wesleyan chapel took place on Sunday. The church was tastefully decorated with flowers' and fruit by the ladies of the congregation. The Rev,. J. Chamberlain,of Didsbury College, was the preacher at both services. There were large congregations through- out the day. ALLEGED ASSAULT BY FARM LABOURERS.—OR Tuesday morning before Messrs. Watkin and Norris, at the Police Station, Martin Cunningham, Thomas Doyle, and Owen. Cunningham. farm labourers, Lymm, were charged with assaulting John Fallow, farm labourer, High Legh, on the 15th September.—Sufficient evidence was given by the police to justify a remand until Friday's Petty Ses- , sions.—The men were allowed out on bail. ACCIDENT TO A FOOTBALLER.—An unfortunate incident took place on Saturday. While returning to the head- quarters of the Lymm Club, one of the members of a picked team which had met the Lvmm club that after- noon, in running down the lane to the village with his comrades fell and broke his arm. He was taken to Dr. Williamson's surgiry who set the limb, after which he , returned home.
